{"qid": "bioasq/5e4946bf6d0a277941000005", "question": "Are genomic regulatory blocks (GRBs) any different than TADs?", "options": {"A": "yes", "B": "no"}, "answer_choice": "A", "justification": "Genomic regulatory blocks (GRBs) and topologically associating domains (TADs) are distinct but related concepts. GRBs are defined by clusters of highly conserved noncoding elements (HCNEs) that regulate developmental genes and maintain conserved synteny across species (Documents [0][1][2]). TADs, on the other hand, are 3D structural units of the genome that restrict chromatin interactions to insulate genes from external regulatory elements (Documents [6][12][24]). While GRBs often coincide with TAD boundaries and exhibit similar conservation (Document [0]), they differ in their functional focus: GRBs emphasize regulatory elements and gene order conservation, whereas TADs emphasize spatial organization and insulation of interactions. This distinction is supported by evidence that TAD boundaries are evolutionarily constrained and structurally distinct from the regulatory landscapes of GRBs (Documents [16][24]).", "true_answer": "B", "is_correct": false, "error_type": null}
